# bench-data.json keys

Generated by `fixtures/store-bench/report-data.mjs` from the result files pinned in `fixtures/store-bench/results/report-sources.json`. Do not edit by hand.

Cite a number as `<span data-n="KEY"></span>`, and a generated block as `<!-- gen:NAME -->`. `build-docs.mjs` fills both from `bench-data.json`, and G5 (`tools/check-numbers.mjs`) fails on any other number in the prose.

**Every src page must include, by hand (build-docs.mjs does not insert them):**

- `<!-- gen:charts-assets -->` once, in the `<head>`: the chart CSS and runtime. Without it the charts render statically, with no filters or tooltips.
- `<!-- gen:build-label -->` near the top: the build provenance line. G5 fails on any page that has gen blocks or `data-n` spans and no build label.

Build: Measured on React 0.0.0-experimental-a3d8e745-20261101 (a3d8e745), compiler c59ecf06d45b47db, results pinned 2026-11-02.

Headline bar: store + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand (`HEADLINE_OPPONENTS` in report-data.mjs). The granular control (zustand-granular, a Zustand store per entity) has its own scoreboard column (`main.tally.zustand-granular.*`), per-cell verdicts (`main.*.vsGranular`) and losses area (`losses.granular.*`). The build without the same-element bailout has its own keys (`nobailout.*`) and generator (`nobailout-table`), never mixed into the headline.

## Generators (`<!-- gen:NAME -->`)

| name | what it draws |
|---|---|
| `stat-tiles` | A row of 3–5 hero numbers. Args: {"keys": [...], "labels": [...], "hrefs": [...], "subs": [...]}. A tally base key (e.g. main.tally.selectors.all) shows matchOrBeat/total with its range (subs "range", the default) or "split" (faster · tie · slower), plus a status icon and words for its losses; any other key shows its display, with an optional sub-line key. Or {"tiles": [{"label", "value", "of", "note", "href"}]}: value and of are keys ("value/of"), note is a key or digit-free words. Needs charts-assets {"extras": true}. |
| `verdict-grid` | Win / tie / loss (icon and words) for every scenario × size × throttle. Args: {"comparison": "headline" | "granular" | "ctx-<off|on>-<vsContext|vsContextSplit>", "compact": true}. Needs charts-assets {"extras": true}. |
| `ctx-scoreboard` | Store vs Context at the same compiler setting: per scenario and overall, store vs one context and vs a context per slice, compiler off and on (ctx.tally.*). |
| `ctx-chart` | Interactive dot plot of use(store), one context and a context per slice in two panels (with and without the compiler, each on its own log axis), by scenario, rows and CPU, with the same-setting verdicts in the note and a table view. Args: {"title", "compact"}. |
| `results-table` | js-framework-benchmark style results. Args: {"suite": "main" | "nobailout" | "memory" | "typing" | "typing-suite" | "fairness5" | "fairness" | "derived-selectors" | "rapid-dispatch" | "many-components-same-slice" | "deeptree-nested-hooks" | "sweeps" | "spike", "columns": [variant ids, or "store+on" / "store+off" for one compiler setting], "rows": {"scenarios": [...], "throttle": [...], "sizes": [...]}, "title", "note"}. Implementations in columns (grouped by compiler setting), configurations in rows grouped by scenario with plain-language descriptions, each cell the mean ± 95% confidence interval (1.96 standard errors of the pooled samples; median and run range for memory and typing) and the slowdown against the fastest cell in its row with the same compiler setting, colored by slowdown (green to red, with a scale), geometric means of the slowdowns at the bottom. Click a row to sort the columns by it; compare under a column tests the others against it with the verdict rule (two standard errors and restart agreement where restart data exists). Needs charts-assets {"extras": true, "results": true}. |
| `speedup-chart` | use(store)'s speedup in every configuration of one comparison: one row per scenario, one mark per size (lane) and CPU throttle (circle or diamond) on a symmetric log-ratio axis around parity, placed by the ratio of means the verdict uses, blue faster, red slower, hollow gray a tie under method.verdictRule, the scenario tally (matched or beaten) at the right, a tooltip with both means and medians and the restart agreement, and a table view. No controls. Args: {"comparison": "headline" | "granular" | "ctx-<off|on>-<vsContext|vsContextSplit>" | "nobailout", "title", "compact"}. Needs charts-assets {"extras": true} only in compact mode. |
| `ctx-compiler-gain` | What the compiler buys store, one context and a context per slice: with vs without it, per scenario and overall (ctx.tally.compilerGain.*). |
| `ctx-counts` | Counting build, one update: counted renders (method.countedComponents) and Row renders for store, one context and a context per slice, compiler off and on, per scenario. |
| `charts-assets` | The chart CSS and runtime (filters, tooltips). Put it once per page, in the `<head>`. {"extras": true} adds the styles for stat-tiles, verdict-grid and compact cards. Compact (card) mode, {"compact": true}, works on scoreboard, scoreboard-controls, main-chart, counts-table (also {"scenarios": [...]}, and in compact {"variants": [...]}: one row per scenario), scoreboard (also {"columns": [comparison ids]}), main-verdicts (compact is the verdict grid), ctx-counts, sweep-chart, spike-chart, the suite-* tables, suite-fairness5, nobailout-table, typing-table, typing-suite-table, memory-chart, losses-table, ctx-scoreboard, ctx-chart, ctx-compiler-gain, verdict-grid and speedup-chart. Compact charts are drawn about 584 wide (a half-width dashboard card), so their text renders at about 12px, and never grow past that. |
| `build-label` | The build provenance line (React version and sha, compiler hash, pin date), or a placeholder warning. G5 requires it on every page that cites numbers. |
| `scoreboard` | Main suite scoreboard: store + compiler vs the faster of Redux/Zustand, and vs each library and context, per scenario and overall; the granular control in its own last column, never in the headline. Low-signal scenarios (structure) are marked. |
| `scoreboard-controls` | Main suite controls: store-single vs selectors, everything without the compiler, and store with vs without the compiler. |
| `main-chart` | Interactive dot plot of the main suite: every variant in two panels (with and without the compiler, each on its own log axis), median and middle half, filterable by scenario, rows and CPU, with the verdict in the note and a table view. Args: {"title", "compact"}. |
| `main-verdicts` | Main suite verdict grid: store + compiler vs the faster of Redux/Zustand for every scenario × size × CPU. |
| `main-table` | Main suite medians: one table per scenario, every variant and compiler setting × size × CPU. |
| `counts-table` | Work for one update in the counting build (compiled): counted renders (only the instrumented components, method.countedComponents), Row renders, selector calls. |
| `suite-derived-selectors` | Redux suite derived-selectors: medians and store / store-memo verdicts. |
| `suite-rapid-dispatch` | Redux suite rapid-dispatch: medians and the store verdict. |
| `suite-many-components-same-slice` | Redux suite many-components-same-slice: medians and the store verdict. |
| `suite-deeptree-nested-hooks` | Redux suite deeptree-nested-hooks: medians and the store verdict. |
| `suite-fairness` | Fairness controls (redux-plain, redux-nofreeze) at N=1,000: medians, and each control vs redux (suite.fairness.control.*). |
| `suite-fairness5` | Fairness with every variant (set fairness5): medians, store + compiler vs the fastest Redux setup (redux, redux-plain, redux-nofreeze) and vs Zustand per configuration, with tallies (suite.fairness5.*). |
| `spike-chart` | Shared derived data spike: a slopegraph of ms per update vs readers per category (the pinned K values, spike.ks; K=spike.defaultK is the derived-selectors suite) for use(store), weakMemo (store-memo) and Redux as the reference, for one update family (mixed by default), with a toggle to one panel per family. role (an unread update) and the other variants are only in the table view. CPU filter. Args: {"family", "title", "compact"}. |
| `sweep-chart` | Sweeps at N=1,000: use(store) and keyed selection (store-keyed) in color, Redux and Zustand in gray, on a linear axis from zero, by compiler coverage (placed by the share compiled) or props per row. Selection by default, entity behind the scenario filter. CPU filter, table view with verdicts. Args: {"title", "compact"}. |
| `typing-table` | Typing latency in the main app (p50, p95, max, and when the list caught up with the input) per variant and CPU. |
| `typing-suite-table` | Typing latency with the useDeferredValue variants of Redux and Zustand. |
| `memory-chart` | Bytes per row after mount (bars, compiler on), filterable by rows, with mount time in the tooltip and a table view. Args: {"title", "compact"}. |
| `nobailout-table` | Without the same-element bailout (react-builds/head-nobailout): the tally vs the headline opponents next to the head build, main-suite medians, store vs the headline opponents and vs the granular control, and what the bailout buys store (bailoutGain); plus the sweeps when pinned. Separate from the headline. |
| `losses-table` | Where it doesn't win: every comparison the store side loses under the verdict rule (method.verdictRule), by area (main vs the headline opponents, main vs the granular control, suites, sweeps, spike), each with the counting build's counted renders, selector calls and derivations for both sides. |

## Comparisons (`main.tally.<comparison>`)

| comparison | meaning |
|---|---|
| `selectors` | store + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand |
| `redux` | store + compiler vs Redux |
| `zustand` | store + compiler vs Zustand |
| `zustand-granular` | store + compiler vs the granular control (a Zustand store per entity) |
| `context` | store + compiler vs one context |
| `context-split` | store + compiler vs a context per slice |
| `single-selectors` | store-single (one store, no selectors) + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand |
| `nocompiler-selectors` | store without the compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand without the compiler |
| `compiler-gain` | store + compiler vs store without the compiler |

Verdict rule (`method.verdictRule`, applied to every verdict and tally): A tie is a difference in means within two standard errors. A larger difference is a win or a loss only if every browser restart agrees on the direction (the side that is faster in the pooled means is faster in each restart, restarts paired by restart number). Otherwise it is a tie. Each library is compared at its faster compiler setting unless the comparison says otherwise. An update no variant renders anything for (the counting build) is an "unread update", counted as no work, never as a win.

History (`history.*`) comes from sets with status `history` in report-sources.json: older runs from other builds, kept to say how the headline moved, outside the head check. The gate keys (`method.gate.*`) come from report-sources.json `gate`: the per-attempt ambient-CPU records and the units logs.

## Keys by pattern

### `build.label`

The build provenance line: React version and sha, compiler hash, when the results were pinned. Placeholder text while any set is not from the frozen build.

- `build.label`: Measured on React 0.0.0-experimental-a3d8e745-20261101 (a3d8e745), compiler c59ecf06d45b47db, results pinned 2026-11-02.

### `build.sha`

Short git sha of the frozen React build the results were measured on.

- `build.sha`: a3d8e745

### `build.version`

React version string of the frozen build.

- `build.version`: 0.0.0-experimental-a3d8e745-20261101

### `build.compiler`

First 16 hex chars of the sha256 of the compiler dist used for the compiled bundles.

- `build.compiler`: c59ecf06d45b47db

### `build.pinnedAt`

The date the result files were pinned in report-sources.json.

- `build.pinnedAt`: 2026-11-02

### `build.status`

"head" when every set is from the frozen build, else "placeholder".

- `build.status`: head

### `method.cpu`

CPU of the benchmark machine.

- `method.cpu`: Apple M4 Max

### `method.browser`

Chromium version (headless shell).

- `method.browser`: 145.0.7632.6

### `method.node`

Node version that ran the harness.

- `method.node`: v25.4.0

### `method.iterations`

Timed samples per configuration and restart in the main suite.

- `method.iterations`: 200

### `method.warmup`

Warm-up updates before timing, main suite.

- `method.warmup`: 50

### `method.restarts`

Browser restarts (interleaved runs) per configuration, main suite.

- `method.restarts`: 3

### `method.samples`

Timed samples per configuration in the main suite, across restarts (median over configurations).

- `method.samples`: 600

### `method.configs`

Main-suite configurations: scenarios × sizes × CPU throttles.

- `method.configs`: 42

### `method.sizes`

Row counts in the main suite, e.g. "100 / 1,000 / 10,000".

- `method.sizes`: 100 / 1,000 / 10,000

### `method.throttles`

CPU throttles in the main suite, e.g. "1× and 4×".

- `method.throttles`: 1× and 4×

### `method.verdictRule`

How every store-vs-opponent verdict and tally is decided (VERDICT_RULE in report-suites.mjs): two standard errors, then the restart-agreement rule.

- `method.verdictRule`: A tie is a difference in means within two standard errors. A larger difference is a win or a loss only if every browser restart agrees on the direction (the side that is faster in the pooled means is faster in each restart, restarts paired by restart number). Otherwise it is a tie.

### `method.gate.ambientStart`

Ambient-CPU gate: a timed unit starts only when the cores in use outside the benchmark (ambient-cpu.mjs, three samples averaged) are at or below this.

- `method.gate.ambientStart`: 3.5

### `method.gate.ambientDiscard`

Ambient-CPU gate: a timed unit is discarded and re-run when the mean ambient cores during it were above this.

- `method.gate.ambientDiscard`: 4.5

### `method.gate.<build>.timedFiles`

Every pinned timed file (counts excluded), one gated unit each: ambientGated + loadGated. build is head or nobailout.

- `method.gate.head.timedFiles`: 100
- `method.gate.nobailout.timedFiles`: 45

### `method.gate.<build>.ambientGated`

Pinned timed files with an ambient-CPU record, i.e. run under the ambient gate.

- `method.gate.head.ambientGated`: 86
- `method.gate.nobailout.ambientGated`: 45

### `method.restartsDefault`

Main suite: the usual number of restarts per configuration.

- `method.restartsDefault`: 3

### `method.restartsExtra`

Main suite: the scenario × throttle units with more restarts than usual, as text, or "none".

- `method.restartsExtra`: selection at 1× CPU and subscribe churn at 1× CPU (5 restarts, every size)

### `method.restartsExtraCount`

Main suite: the restarts the extra units got (when they all got the same number).

- `method.restartsExtraCount`: 5

### `method.restartsExtraAdded`

Main suite: restarts added to each extra unit (restartsExtraCount − restartsDefault).

- `method.restartsExtraAdded`: 2

### `method.verifySteps`

The DOM check (run.mjs --verify): updates replayed per scenario before comparing every bundle's DOM.

- `method.verifySteps`: 20

### `nobailout.main.flips`

Which headline verdicts the same-element bailout changes, as text: configuration, verdict without it, verdict with it.

- `nobailout.main.flips`: selection at N=10,000, 1× CPU (loss without the bailout, tie with it), selection at N=10,000, 4× CPU (tie without the bailout, win with it), subscribe churn at N=1,000, 1× CPU (tie without the bailout, win with it), and subscribe churn at N=1,000, 4× CPU (loss without the bailout, win with it)

### `nobailout.main.flipCount`

How many headline verdicts the bailout changes.

- `nobailout.main.flipCount`: 4

### `nobailout.main.flipScenarios`

Where the bailout changes headline verdicts, in lowercase prose names, e.g. "two in selection and two in subscribe churn".

- `nobailout.main.flipScenarios`: two in selection and two in subscribe churn

### `method.restartsExtraUnits`

Main suite: how many scenario × throttle units had more restarts than usual.

- `method.restartsExtraUnits`: 2

### `method.gate.history`

One line: how the gate changed during the pinned runs, from the gate lines of the units logs.

- `method.gate.history`: Load-average gate first (start at or below a 1-minute load, re-run if the load after the unit was above: 4/6, then 6/8, then 5/8, with structure units re-run above 12), used by main restart 1 (14 files). Then from 21:37 an ambient-CPU gate on cores used outside the benchmark (start at or below 3, re-run if the mean was above 4, set to 3.5/4.5 at 21:39), used by every other pinned timed file.

### `counts.<suite>.<scenario>.<variant>.<compiler>.<metric>`

Counting build, one update (averaged where the suite averages): renders (counted renders: only components that call countRender; for main and fairness, method.countedComponents, so App and the list aren't counted), rows (Row renders), selectors (selector calls) or derivations. Suite "main" is the main suite at method.countsRows rows.

### `losses.scope`

One line: what the losses table counts and which control comparisons it leaves out.

- `losses.scope`: The losses table counts the headline (store + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand), the granular control, the ported Redux suites, the fastest Redux setup in the fairness run, the sweeps, and the shared derived data spike. It leaves out the controls: store + compiler vs one context (main), store + compiler vs a context per slice (main), one store without selectors (store-single) + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand (main), store vs the faster of Redux and Zustand, everything without the compiler (main), store + compiler vs the faster of Redux and Zustand on the build without the same-element bailout, and store + compiler vs zustand in the fairness run with every variant.

### `method.headlineRedux`

Which Redux the headline is defined against (idiomatic RTK), and where the leaner setups are compared, for the Method sentence.

- `method.headlineRedux`: The headline compares against idiomatic Redux: Redux Toolkit (configureStore, createSlice, Immer with auto-freeze) and react-redux, as in src/apps/redux.jsx. The leaner setups, plain Redux without RTK or Immer (redux-plain) and RTK without auto-freeze (redux-nofreeze), are compared separately, in the fairness run with every variant, against the fastest of the three.
